## management-governance — module synthesis

# Management-Governance Module — EMAR (Synthesis)

## Abstract

Emaar's operators are competent and have delivered, but alignment with minority holders is structurally thin, so stewardship nets to mixed. Capital allocation is genuinely value-creative: earnings per share roughly quadrupled (AED 0.52 to 1.99), the dividend grew (AED 0.25 to 1.00 per share), and return on capital rose from 4.7% to 13.7%, on a net-cash balance sheet with no value-destruction traps. Incentives are split: a real share-price-linked plan for the CEO and CFO, but a founder-Managing Director paid AED 61m with no share link who owns 0.03%. The biggest risk is RF-OWN-004 — the Government of Dubai (Dubai Holding, 29.73%) controls the company, the board is government-staffed, and an IFRS election hides the largest related-party channel. Verdict: standard / mixed.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** **Standard / mixed** — competent, promise-keeping operators with a value-creative per-share record, held back from "aligned & competent" by structurally thin, government-anchored alignment (0.03% insider ownership, a government controller, a founder-MD who chairs a competitor, and a related-party channel left unquantified).
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** **N** — `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` tested all 8 hard disqualifiers and triggered none (clean EY/KPMG audits 3 yrs, no going concern, control-group pledge 0%, RPT 0.35% rev / 1.2% exp, one disclosed auditor rotation, no restatement, no reporting enforcement, positive operating cash flow every year). Verdict is therefore NOT capped at "Serious governance concerns."
- Management quality /100: **70**
- Capital allocation /100: **70**
- Incentive alignment /100: **50**
- Shareholder friendliness /100: **53**
- Disclosure candor /100: **65** (partial-data cap — no verbatim transcript)
- Governance risk /100 *(higher = WORSE — inverted)*: **58**
- Data quality /100: **72** *(from 00 — "Sufficient")*
- Overall usefulness /100: **78**
- Insider ownership (one line): Directors/insiders own **0.03%** (founder-MD Alabbar 2,700,500 sh); **Government of Dubai via Dubai Holding group controls 29.73%** (single largest holder 22.27%); single share class, **no pledging**, ~70.2% free float [FY2025 AR, CG Report p.125 / Annex I p.153].
- Biggest governance signal (one line): **RF-OWN-004** — a Government-of-Dubai controller (Dubai Holding 29.73%) with a city-building agenda, a board on which every "independent" director is a government official, and an **IAS 24 election that leaves the largest related-party channel** (land / utilities / construction from the state ecosystem) **unquantified to minorities**.
- **Governance Score /100:** **59** — inputs: `0.20×70 (CapAlloc) + 0.18×50 (Incentive) + 0.18×53 (ShFriendliness) + 0.16×65 (Candor) + 0.16×70 (MgmtQuality) + 0.12×(100−58) (GovRisk)` = `14.00 + 9.00 + 9.54 + 10.40 + 11.20 + 5.04 = 59.18 ≈ 59`.
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100:** **41** (= 59 × 70 / 100 = 41.4).
- **Governance Rating:** **Watchlist** (55–69 band; no critical flag or hard disqualifier forces it lower).
- **Confidence Score /100 (source quality):** **70** — most findings rest on Tier-1 audited English annual reports and the CG Report; deducted for the transcript-proxy candor gap, absent AGM vote tallies, and undisclosed incentive-metric weights.
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count:** **2 / 0** (RF-OWN-004 High; RF-MGT-001 High/watch). Two further High-materiality alignment channels are open but did not fire a registry flag on current evidence (founder-MD / Eagle Hills competitor conflict; IAS 24 government-ecosystem RPT opacity).

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation *(CLAUDE.md §8)*

- **Strongest bear point:** The controller is the Government of Dubai (Dubai Holding group 29.73%), whose objective is city development, not minority per-share value; the board is staffed entirely by government officials/executives, and the **IAS 24 government-related-entity election leaves the biggest value-transfer channel — land/utilities/construction bought from the state ecosystem — entirely unquantified** [05-016, 05-021, 04-004]. The AED 7.5bn Dubai Creek Harbour asset was bought *from* parent Dubai Holding, part-funded by issuing new Emaar shares to that same owner, with no arm's-length valuation shown to minorities [02-005]. This is a structural cap (§24 Filter 6), not a discount to arbitrage.
- **Strongest bull point (steelman):** On the capital actually deployed, this team created per-share value and avoided every classic trap — EPS AED 0.52→1.99, DPS AED 0.25→1.00, ROIC 4.7%→13.7%, net cash ~AED 25bn, dividend covered ~2.9–3.7x by free cash flow, **zero** value-destructive M&A spree, no uncovered dividend, no buyback-at-high-prices, and clean straight-IFRS accounting with **no adjusted-earnings engineering** (earnings-quality 81/100) [02-010, 02-012, 02-014, 06-001].
- **Single killer risk (stewardship-specific):** A future asset injected richly from the parent (Dubai Holding) and funded by dilution — the exact structure of the 2022 DCH deal — would flip capital allocation from value-creative to extractive, and the IAS 24 exemption means minorities would not see the price [02-005, 05-016] (§24 Filter 6 owner-conflict).
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible (cutting *against* the bear):** dividends flow **pro-rata** to all holders (~AED 8.84bn, AED 1.00/sh held FY2024–25); **single one-share-one-vote class, no pledging, zero equity dilution**; disclosed RPT is tiny (0.35% rev); and directors executed **no** trades (no selling before news) [02-010, 04-006, 04-007, 04-008, 05-015]. No *proven* extraction exists today — the risk is the channel, not a demonstrated leak.

## 3. Reconciliation

- **Dividend "flat" (01) vs "grew" (02) — resolved in favour of 02 (primary source).** Agent 01 read the dividend as "held flat at AED 1.00/share FY2023–25"; agent 02 corrected this against the **audited FY2023/FY2024/FY2025 AR Note 32**, showing the parent dividend actually **grew** (AED 0.25 → 0.50 → 1.00 → 1.00) and reconciled it to the cash-flow line. The corrected reading is a **stronger** per-share story, not weaker; I adopt 02's audited figure. No score change (both readings support "value-creative").
- **Capital-allocation score 82 (prior run) vs 70 (this run) — this run's more conservative read wins.** The current agent 02 marks capital allocation down to 70 for the **related-party Dubai Creek Harbour purchase**, the ~61%-of-CFO cash/securities pile, and cycle dependence — items the prior run's 82 under-weighted. This is a re-scoring toward conservatism, not a governance deterioration; I carry 70.
- **"Good operating record" vs "thin alignment" — the defining tension, reconciled to *mixed*.** Agents 01/02 show competent, value-creative operators; agents 04/05 show a government-controlled board and 0.03% insider economics; agent 03 shows a per-share LTIP spine undercut by an unaligned founder-MD packet. These are not contradictory — they describe a competent team operating inside a structurally misaligned ownership frame. Under the conservative default and §24 Filter 6 ("a structural cap, not a discount to arbitrage"), the alignment cap sets the ceiling: overall stewardship nets to **Standard / mixed**, and shareholder friendliness is held at 53 (agent 05's board/rights read, the definitional match and the more conservative of 04's 57 and 05's 53, under the Filter-6 max-55 cap).
- **Language-driven caps from the prior run NOT inherited (CLAUDE.md §27).** The 2026-07-03 run capped incentive alignment for "no executive comp in English" and softened shareholder friendliness on similar grounds. All Emaar filings are in English (triage 00) and the current specialists read the CG Report + Annex D directly, so **no language cap applies**; incentive alignment 50 and shareholder friendliness 53 are substantive scores, not language-gapped.
- **RF-DISC-001 / RF-DISC-002 (fired in the prior run) NOT triggered this run.** Agent 06 tested both and found them *approached but not triggered* (margin level still ~54%; no recurring earnings add-backs). The current disclosure red-flag set is therefore narrower and better-evidenced.

## 5. Stewardship Summary

These people have largely done what they said: property sales more than doubled to AED 80.4bn, backlog compounded to AED 163.4bn, deliveries beat plan, and the dividend grew from AED 0.25 to AED 1.00 per share — a genuine kept-promise record, though flattered by a Dubai upcycle, legacy cheap land, and a FY2025 tax one-off, so it reads as cycle-strong execution rather than proof of through-cycle skill. Capital has created per-share value: EPS roughly quadrupled and ROIC rose from 4.7% to 13.7% on a net-cash balance sheet, with every classic value-destruction trap avoided (no debt-funded M&A spree, no high-price buybacks, no uncovered dividend). But incentives and ownership point only partly at per-share value — a real share-price-linked plan covers the CEO and CFO, yet the founder-MD takes AED 61m with no share link while owning 0.03%, and the whole team's economic skin in the game is de minimis. Minority protection is the weak axis: the board complies with UAE code on paper (55.6% independent, one-share-one-vote, no dilution) but is not independent of the *controller* — every "independent" director is a Dubai-government official, and the IAS 24 election leaves the largest related-party channel (land, utilities, construction from the state ecosystem) unquantified. The single most important reason to withhold full trust is not any proven abuse — dividends are pro-rata, there is no pledging and no adjusted-earnings games — it is that the controller is a government whose objective is Dubai's development, not minority per-share value, which caps how far competence and a clean balance sheet can carry the thesis.

## 5B. Peer Governance Benchmark

Peers from `business-model/08_competitive-map`: **Aldar (ADX, listed)**, **DAMAC (private since 2022)**, **Sobha (private)**. Governance-specific metrics are largely undisclosed for the two private peers; Aldar is the only listed comparator and is itself state-linked (Mubadala/government), so the UAE developer norm is state/family control — "in line" here is a weak-norm benchmark, not a minority-protective one.

| Metric | Company (EMAR) | Peer Median | Peer Verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Board independence % | 55.6% (legal); 0 truly independent of the controller | Not disclosed (Aldar state-linked; DAMAC/Sobha private) | In line (weak UAE norm) — but true independence-from-owner worse than a dispersed-ownership benchmark |
| Insider / promoter holding % | Insiders 0.03%; government controller 29.73% | Aldar government/Mubadala-linked; DAMAC founder-owned (private); Sobha group-owned | In line (state/family control is the sector norm) |
| Pledge % | 0% | Not disclosed | In line / not assessable |
| Non-audit / audit fee ratio | Not quantified in pool | Not disclosed | Not assessed |
| RPT intensity (% of revenue) | 0.35% disclosed (IAS 24 leaves govt-ecosystem unquantified) | Not disclosed | Not comparable (disclosure regimes differ) |
| AGM votes-against (key resolutions) | Not in pool | Not in pool | Not assessed |

*Net: on the mechanical axes Emaar is in line with a UAE sector where state/family control is normal; the governance gap that matters (a board that checks the controller for minorities) is a sector-wide weakness, not an Emaar-specific outlier — but "typical" is not "minority-protective."*

## 5D. Analyst Follow-Up Questions

- **RF-OWN-004 (government controller):** Are there any Emaar↔Dubai-Holding-ecosystem asset transactions planned that would repeat the DCH structure (asset in, shares out)? Would management itemise or range the IAS 24-exempt land/utilities/construction flows? Is any director genuinely independent of the government? (Material to minority value; recurring; affects minority holders directly.)
- **RF-MGT-001 (CFO exit):** Was Heikal's departure performance-, strategy-, or new-controller-driven? Does Chindalia move past "until further notice" to a permanent appointment? (One-off vs pattern; company-specific; disclosure adequacy in question.)
- **Founder-MD / Eagle Hills conflict:** Is Emaar↔Eagle Hills deal-flow ring-fenced, and is any competitive overlap disclosed to minorities? (Recurring channel; affects minority holders; management explanation not yet on record.)
- **Founder-MD pay:** Is the AED 36m bonus tied to any per-share/returns metric, and why is the MD excluded from the share-price LTIP? Recurring run-rate of the AED 13.3m travel/logistics line? (Material to alignment, not aggregate size.)
- **Q4'25 margin fade (RF-DISC-001 approached):** Does the FY2025 AR MD&A quantify the segment-margin decline that the press release framed as "healthy margins"? (Candor test; recurring at each print.)
- **AGM dissent:** Pull DFM AGM scrutinizer/vote tallies to test say-on-pay and RPT-resolution support (currently Insufficient Data).

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- **Stewardship verdict = Standard / mixed.** Strongest single evidence: competent, promise-keeping operators (sales AED 80.4bn, backlog AED 163.4bn, dividend AED 0.25→1.00/sh all kept) whose alignment is structurally thin — 0.03% insider economics under a Government-of-Dubai controller (Dubai Holding 29.73%).
- **Capital allocation is value-creative (per share).** EPS AED 0.52→1.99, DPS AED 0.25→1.00, ROIC 4.7%→13.7%, net cash ~AED 25bn, dividend covered ~2.9–3.7x by FCF, zero value-destruction traps. Worst single item: the AED 7.5bn Dubai Creek Harbour asset bought *from* the controlling parent, part-paid in new Emaar shares, no arm's-length price shown to minorities.
- **Incentives / ownership only partly align with minorities.** A genuine share-price-linked LTIP for the CEO/CFO (~75% at-risk) sits beside a founder-MD paid AED 61m with no share link who owns 0.03%; whole-team skin in the game is de minimis; dividends and votes are pro-rata / one-share-one-vote.
- **Biggest governance risk = RF-OWN-004 (High, not Critical).** Government controller + government-staffed board + IAS 24 opacity over the largest related-party channel. No *proven* extraction today (pro-rata dividends, no pledging, clean audit, net cash) — the risk is the channel and the controller's objective, treated per §24 Filter 6 as a structural cap and a **value-trap note to valuation**, not a discount to arbitrage.
- **Hard disqualifier flagged by business-model/01?** **No** — verbatim: "No verdict-lock. None of the 8 hard disqualifiers is triggered." Governance risk is NOT floored at 80; verdict is NOT locked at "Serious governance concerns."
- **§24 rejector filters:** only **Filter 6 (RF-OWN-004)** tripped — cap applied: shareholder friendliness ≤55 (landed 53), governance-risk floor 55 (landed 58), value-trap note to valuation. Filters 1 (integrity — cleared), 2 (turnaround — N/A), 4 (serial acquirer — N/A) did NOT trip.
- **Partial-data cap:** the disclosure-candor cap (max 65) binds — no verbatim transcript in the pool (only FAB sell-side proxies, which do not lift the cap), so verbal Q&A tone / owning-the-miss-live are Not assessable. It limits only the candor read, not the numbers-based reads.
- **Biggest missing data point (single highest-value next request):** the **IAS 24 government-ecosystem related-party detail** (land / utilities / construction / financing flows with the Dubai-government sphere) — the one channel that could flip capital allocation from value-creative to extractive and the largest blind spot for a minority holder. (Second: a verbatim earnings transcript to lift the candor cap.)
- **Explicit handoff:** this module is the governance deep-dive and **supersedes** the `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance` quick-read. The master synthesizer should treat **this module's governance verdict (Standard / mixed) and scores (Governance Score 59 / Watchlist; Confidence-Adjusted 41)** as the **primary governance read**.

## 8. Simple Summary

- **Promises:** Mostly kept — sales, backlog, deliveries and a rising dividend all came through, but the record leaned on a strong Dubai property cycle and a one-off tax break.
- **Capital → per-share value:** Yes — earnings per share roughly quadrupled and returns on capital nearly tripled, on a debt-free (net-cash) balance sheet, with no value-destroying deals or buybacks.
- **What the pay rewards:** Split — the CEO and CFO have a real share-price-linked plan; the founder-boss takes ~AED 61m (three times the CEO) with no share link and no disclosed formula while owning 0.03%.
- **Skin in the game:** Almost none — insiders own 0.03%; they neither bought nor sold last year; the government (via Dubai Holding) controls 29.73%; no shares are pledged.
- **Minority protection:** Weak where it counts — the board follows the UAE rulebook but every "independent" director is a government official, so it checks management, not the government owner.
- **Candor in bad times:** Mixed — the accounting is clean and honest (no "adjusted" earnings games), but the press releases soft-pedal what's going wrong, and the biggest related-party channel is left unquantified.
- **Hard disqualifier:** None flagged (clean audits, no going concern, no pledging, tiny related-party numbers).
- **Useful for the master synthesizer?** Yes — clear, evidence-backed governance read; this module supersedes the business-model quick-read on governance.

## Language is not a data gap (CLAUDE.md §27)

All Emaar disclosures in this pool are filed in **English** — there is no non-English document and therefore no translation step and no language-driven data gap. Every source was transcribed verbatim by `extract_pool.py` with **0 extraction failures** [`manifest.json`]. The IAS 24 government-related-entity note that leaves most Dubai-Government-ecosystem transactions unquantified is a **disclosed standard election**, not opacity and not a language issue. The only category that would count as truly missing is an extraction-failed document — there are none here.

## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** **Sufficient**
- **Reason:** A proxy-equivalent (AGM Notice + Corporate Governance Report), beneficial-ownership table, board composition/independence, related-party disclosure, director compensation, three years of audited annual reports, and transcripts are all present and extracted cleanly (0 failures), plus a multi-year capital-allocation history — so all six specialists can run.
- **Specialists that can run:** all six — management track record (01), capital allocation (02), incentives (03), ownership & insider behavior (04), board & shareholder rights (05), candor & disclosure (06).
- **Hard disqualifier already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan?** N — no disqualifier triggered. Soft signals routed to this module (not verdict-locking): government/unaligned-owner control (§24 Filter 6), IAS 24 government-related-entity RP-transparency election, India Enforcement Directorate contingency (~0.2% of assets), Group Head of Finance change (20-May-2026, named successor).
- **Active partial-data caps:** none (verdict is Sufficient).
- **Critical missing items:** none that block. Lower-priority gaps for later agents to note (not caps): (1) the CIQ structured insider/ownership export (granular director/officer open-market share-dealing) — incremental value modest given individual insiders hold 0.03% and institutional buy/sell flow is already in the Public Ownership Summary; (2) executive incentive-metric weights (thin in UAE PJSC disclosure — agent 03); (3) detailed AGM vote tallies / scrutinizer results (agent 05).
- **Single highest-value missing document:** the CIQ ownership / insider-transaction structured export (director/officer open-market trades) — the only governance data category not covered by a present source.

# Management & Track Record — EMAR

*Emaar Properties PJSC (DFM: EMAAR). Jurisdiction: UAE / Dubai Financial Market. Reporting standard: IFRS. Currency: AED (dirham, pegged to USD at ~3.6725). Fiscal year ends 31 December. Latest audited year FY2025; latest quarter Q1 2026 (released 11 May 2026). Annual reports are filed in English — no translation gap.*

*Scope note: this agent judges who runs Emaar, whether they are stable, and whether they delivered on what they said. Ownership/insider behaviour (Filter 6, RF-OWN-004), the compensation scorecard, board independence detail, and full disclosure-candor scoring belong to agents 03/04/05/06 and are referenced only as context here.*

## 1. Key Executives

| Role | Name | Appointed | Tenure | Relevant Background | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Group CEO | Amit Jain, CA, CFA | Group CEO since **June 2017** (joined Emaar 2006; prior Group COO, CEO Emaar Dubai from Dec 2014, former Group CFO) | ~9 yrs as CEO; ~20 yrs at Emaar | Chartered Accountant (ICAI) and CFA charterholder; banking / audit / consulting; ran Emaar's India JVs | CIQ Professionals export, data as of 2026-06-28; FY2025 AR (IFRS), Annex D — Senior Executive Employees |
| Group CFO / Group Head of Finance | Pawan Chindalia | **20 May 2026** | ~2 months (new) | Internal promotion — was Head of Finance for listed subsidiary Emaar Development PJSC; still holds that role "until further notice" | Key Developments (DFM intimation), 2026-05-22; CIQ Professionals export, 2026-06-28 |
| Founder / Managing Director | H.E. Mohamed Ali Rashed Alabbar | Founder **1997**; MD since **Dec 2020** (was Chairman 1997–Dec 2020) | Founder, 29 yrs; MD ~5.5 yrs | Founder of Emaar and of Dubai Financial Market; Director-General of Dubai's Dept. of Economic Development since 1992; **Chairman of Eagle Hills (a competing global developer)**, Americana, Noon.com; owns only ~0.03% of Emaar | CIQ Professionals export, 2026-06-28; FY2025 AR (IFRS), Corporate Governance Report — Board bios |
| Chairman (Non-Executive, **Non-Independent**) | Jamal Majed Khalfan Bin Thaniah | Dec 2020 | ~5.5 yrs | Co-founder of DP World; Dubai-government / ports pedigree (retired from DP World Jan 2017) | FY2025 AR (IFRS), CG Report — Board bios; Compensation Summary export |
| Executive Director, Group Operations (COO function) | Ahmed Al Falasi | Dec 2014 | ~11.5 yrs | Long-serving operator; core team on Burj Khalifa / The Dubai Mall / Dubai Fountain | CIQ Professionals export, 2026-06-28 |
| CEO, Strategic Projects | Maitha Al Dossari | 2000 (this role more recent) | ~26 yrs at Emaar | Ran Emaar Retail/Entertainment; corporate services | CIQ Professionals export, 2026-06-28; FY2025 AR, Annex D |

**Founder / owner-operator status.** Emaar is *founder-led by role* — Alabbar founded it in 1997 and is Managing Director — but it is **not owner-operated by economics.** He holds only **2,700,500 shares (~0.03%)** and all individuals/insiders together hold **0.03%** of the company [FY2025 AR, CG Report — director shareholding; Public Ownership Summary]. Real control sits with the **Government of Dubai** (Dubai Holding group ~29.73% after the May-2026 block transfer; see §2). So the founder's alignment is reputational, not a large personal stake — and he simultaneously chairs a competing developer, Eagle Hills. The Group CEO (Jain) is a long-tenured professional manager, not an owner.

## 2. Management Stability

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Senior departures (last 3 yrs) | **Group CFO/Head of Finance Hesham Heikal "left the company" effective 20 May 2026**, announced 22 May (2 days *after* the effective date), **no reason disclosed**; named internal successor appointed same day. Board Secretary changed Dec 2025 (Mohamed AlQahtani in). Long-serving Executive Director Ahmad Al Matrooshi stepped off the board in 2024. Several independent directors rotated off in 2024 (Al Ali, Al Mansoori, Al Marri). | Key Developments, 2026-05-22; CIQ Professionals & Compensation exports, 2026-06-28 |
| CFO turnover | **One change in the period, after a ~5.5-yr tenure** — Heikal was Group CFO from 22 Nov 2020 to 20 May 2026, so this is **not serial CFO churn**. Mitigants: orderly, named internal successor (Chindalia, ex-Emaar Development). Concerns: reason undisclosed, announced after the fact, and it landed **9 days after the controlling 22.27% block changed hands** (ICD → Emirates Power / Dubai Holding, completed 11 May 2026) — timing worth watching. | FY2025 AR, Annex D — Senior Executive Employees (appointment date 22 Nov 2020); Key Developments, 2026-05-11 & 2026-05-22 |
| CEO succession / continuity | **Strong continuity at the top:** CEO Jain stable ~9 yrs; founder-MD Alabbar 29 yrs; Chairman Bin Thaniah and MD Alabbar both in place since the Dec-2020 reshuffle. Deep, named C-suite: General Counsel (Hamdy), Group Head IT (Khalil), CEO Strategic Projects (Al Dossari), segment heads for Development, Projects, Hospitality, Malls & Entertainment, and Group Head of Internal Audit (Alshaikh). **But no CEO/MD succession plan is disclosed**, and the franchise leans on founder Alabbar (key-person risk). | FY2025 AR, Annex C — Organisational Structure; CIQ Professionals export, 2026-06-28 |
| Control / governance flux | The Dubai-government controlling block was **re-shuffled in May 2026** (ICD's 22.27% sold to Emirates Power Investment LLC for AED 23.9bn; Dubai Holding group total to 29.73%). A new controller can reshape the board and finance leadership — the abrupt CFO change may be an early sign. Routed to agent 04 (RF-OWN-004, government-owner). | Key Developments, 2026-05-11; business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md |

**Warning flagged.** The abrupt, unexplained Group CFO exit is an **Amber** watch item (trigger type **RF-MGT-001**). It does **not**, on current evidence, rise to a Critical governance red flag — the prior CFO had a multi-year tenure, the successor is a named internal hire, and there is no corroborating distress (clean EY FY2025 audit, positive operating cash flow every year, no restatement, no going-concern) [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md]. Follow-up: was the departure performance-, strategy-, or new-controller-driven, and does the successor stay past "until further notice"?

## 3. Stated Strategy & Targets

Emaar's plan is to keep converting cheap legacy Dubai land into branded master-planned communities sold off-plan, funded by buyer instalments, wrapped around a recurring mall-and-hospitality annuity. **It issues no formal numeric (revenue/EBITDA/EPS) guidance** [earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md] and steers instead with operational signposts and policy statements. The explicit, checkable targets management has set: **~AED 65bn of capex over five years**; **gross margin "low 50s sustainably"** over the medium term; **dividend policy "unchanged … current payout in the near term"** (100% of share capital, AED 1.00/share); **50,800+ units under construction for delivery over 5–6 years** with deliveries "ramping materially 2026–28"; and revenue backlog expected to "continue to grow or remain stable unless a sustained slowdown in sales" [FAB Securities "Earnings Call Insight", 4Q25 (paraphrasing the call; unverified); Q4/FY2025 press release, 12 Feb 2026]. The headline strategic signal is scale-at-the-top-of-cycle: an **AED 200bn single-district masterplan** (4.5m sqm GFA, ~150,000 residents) unveiled June 2026, plus Dubai Square Mall and a Dubai Mall expansion by 2H28 [Key Developments, 2026-06-11].

## 4. Promise vs Delivery

| Prior Promise / Target | Period Set | Actual Outcome | Kept / Missed | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Grow property sales (the leading indicator) | FY2023 baseline AED 37.4bn | FY2025 **AED 80.4bn (+15.6% YoY)**; Q1 2026 AED 22.4bn (+16%) — more than doubled in two years | **Kept** | FY2023 AR (IFRS), Performance Review (AED 37.4bn); Q4/FY2025 & Q1 2026 press releases |
| Keep revenue backlog growing/stable | FY2023 baseline AED 62.1bn | AED 155bn at Dec-2025 (+39% YoY); **AED 163.4bn at 31 Mar 2026 (+29% YoY)** | **Kept** | FY2023 AR, Performance Review; Q4/FY2025 & Q1 2026 press releases |
| Maintain dividend at 100% of share capital (AED 1.00/sh) | FY2023 policy | AED 1.00/share held **FY2023, FY2024, FY2025**; ~AED 8.84bn paid 2026; policy reaffirmed "unchanged" | **Kept** | FY2025 AR, Note 32 (Dividends); Q4/FY2025 press release; business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md |
| Deliver units on schedule | FY2025 delivery plan | **6,129 units delivered in 2025, "exceeding initial expectations"**; >129,100 delivered since 2002 | **Kept (beat)** | FAB Securities "Earnings Call Insight", 4Q25 (unverified); Q1 2026 press release |
| Grow the business (multi-year operating record) | FY2021 baseline | Revenue **AED 27.9bn→49.6bn**, EBITDA **7.8bn→24.1bn**, ROIC **4.7%→12.5%** (13.7% LTM) over FY2021–FY2025 | **Kept (cycle-aided)** | ciq_facts.json (multi_year_trajectory); CIQ Financials → Income Statement/Ratios |
| Clear the Street's bar | Rolling consensus | **Beat EPS in 4 of last 5 FYs** (only FY2022 −6%); revenue beat 4 of 5 (FY2022 −4%); beat 3 of last 4 quarters | **Mostly kept** | earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md; ciq_facts.json (surprise_history) |
| "Low 50s" gross margin, sustainably | 4Q25 (medium-term) | FY2025 ~55%; Q4'25 55.4% and declining on mix (−251 bps YoY EBITDA margin); Street models 52.7%→50.9%→47.7% | **On track, but fading** — a forward promise to watch | Q4'25 earnings-call summary; earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md |

**Read on delivery.** The checkable record is **mostly kept, and it rests on real numbers, not narrative** — sales, backlog, deliveries, dividend and returns all moved the way management said. Three honest qualifiers keep this from being an unblemished record: (1) the delivery **coincided with a strong Dubai property upcycle** (base-rate discipline, §9) — margins/ROIC are flattered by legacy cheap land and by a FY2025 one-off tax relief, and consensus already models an earnings peak ~2029 then decline (LT-growth consensus −14.8%) [business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md; earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md]; (2) **revenue misses on timing recur** even in good years (Q1 2026 −2.4%, Q2 2025 −4.6%, percentage-of-completion) [earnings/04]; and (3) promises are **operational signposts, not audited numeric guidance**, and no verbatim call transcript exists in the pool (only sell-side proxies) — so the bar management is held to is one it sets loosely itself.

## 4A. Turnaround & Integrity Tests (CLAUDE.md §24, Filters 1 & 2)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Is the thesis a turnaround (struggling business + new management / new plan)? | **No.** Emaar is a profitable, cash-generative, net-cash (~AED 25bn) developer with ROIC rising to 13.7% and a 9-year-tenured CEO — a franchise at a cycle high, not a broken business being fixed. | ciq_facts.json (multi_year_trajectory, net_debt); business-model/02_business-identity.md |
| If yes: years of *proven* operating inflection actually delivered | Not applicable — no turnaround claimed or needed. (For completeness, the delivered improvement is real: 5 straight years of rising revenue/EBITDA/ROIC.) | ciq_facts.json |
| Résumé / plan / consultant deck cited as the reason to believe? | No. The read rests on delivered outputs (sales, deliveries, backlog, dividend, returns), not on a pitch. The AED 200bn masterplan is a plan, not evidence — credited only as forward capital commitment, not as delivery. | FY2023–FY2025 ARs; Key Developments, 2026-06-11 |
| Routed integrity "buzz" from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (unverified adverse signal) | **None that is an integrity buzz.** The scan found *no proven fraud by controller or senior management* and routed four *disclosed* soft signals: government-owner control, IAS 24 RPT-non-disclosure election, the India Enforcement Directorate land contingency (AED 190m, ~0.2% of assets, disclosed, deemed non-material), and the CFO change. All are disclosed matters, not unverified adverse buzz. | business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md |

**Turnaround rule (Filter 2).** Not a turnaround thesis → **no base-rate turnaround penalty, RF-MGT-004 not triggered, Management Quality not capped at 60 on this ground.** Classify honestly: this is a well-run cyclical developer, not a governance-turnaround.

**Integrity rule (Filter 1).** The integrity signal was chased as far as the pool allows and **cleared**: the only near-integrity items (India ED contingency, the founder-MD's competing Eagle Hills vehicle, the IAS 24 election) are **fully disclosed** in the annual report and sit below hard-disqualifier thresholds; none is an unproven-but-unignorable adverse signal about management defrauding stakeholders. **`RF-MGT-005` is NOT emitted** (no unresolved adverse integrity signal). The founder-MD conflict and government-control alignment issues are real but structural — routed to agents 03/04/05 (compensation, ownership, board), not integrity.

## 5. Management Read

The operating team is **competent and has delivered what it said**: under a stable, 9-year CEO (Amit Jain) and a 29-year founder-MD, Emaar roughly doubled revenue, tripled EBITDA, lifted ROIC from ~5% to ~14%, grew property sales to a record AED 80bn and backlog to AED 163bn, and held its AED 1.00/share dividend across FY2023–25 — a genuine kept-promise record, though one clearly **aided by a Dubai property upcycle and a FY2025 one-off tax relief**, so credit it as cycle-strong execution rather than proof of through-cycle skill. The single biggest management signal is a **governance-alignment gap sitting on top of a good operating record**: control just passed between Dubai-government vehicles (ICD → Dubai Holding, 29.73%), the founder-MD holds only ~0.03% and chairs a **competing** developer, and the **Group CFO left abruptly and unexplained in May 2026** (Amber, RF-MGT-001 watch) days after that control change — competence is proven, but minority-aligned stewardship and finance-leadership stability are the things to watch.

## management-governance / 02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md

_Source: `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`_

# Capital-Allocation Scorecard — EMAR

*Emaar Properties PJSC (DFM: EMAAR). Jurisdiction: UAE / Dubai Financial Market. Reporting standard: IFRS. Reporting currency: AED (dirham, pegged to USD at ~3.6725). Fiscal year ends 31 December. Window: FY2021–FY2025 (5 audited years) + LTM to 31 Mar 2026. Multi-year history is available — this scorecard is NOT single-period, so no partial-data cap applies. Annual reports are filed in English; no translation gap.*

*Scope: this agent grades how management spent shareholders' money over 3–5 years — M&A, buybacks, dividends, capex, debt. Ownership/insider alignment (Filter 6, RF-OWN-004), compensation, and full related-party scoring belong to agents 03/04/05/06 and are referenced only as context. This module runs BEFORE valuation, so buybacks are NOT judged against intrinsic value.*

**Plain-English glossary (first use):** CFO = cash generated by operations; FCF = free cash flow = CFO − capital spending; ROIC = return on invested capital, the profit earned on each AED 100 of capital put to work; incremental ROIC = the return on the *new* capital added over a period; NOPAT = operating profit after tax; NCI = non-controlling interest (the minority holders of a listed subsidiary such as Emaar Development); RPT = related-party transaction; net cash = cash and liquid investments minus total borrowings.

**Reconciliation to the deterministic facts sidecar (`ciq_facts.json`).** My reads tie to the pinned facts: LTM CFO AED 31,973.0m ✓; net cash (broad) AED 24,969.2m ✓; total debt AED 10,064.4m ✓; interest cover 52.1x ✓; shares 8,838.8m ✓; ROIC path 4.7%→13.7% ✓. No material gap. One §15 note: the sidecar's `levered_fcf_m` AED 3,066.7m is CIQ's *levered* FCF (after interest and after netting real-estate/securities investment) — it is NOT the CFO−capex FCF used here and the two are not interchangeable.

---

## 1. Uses of Capital (FY2021–FY2025, cumulative)

Reporting currency: **AED millions.** Denominator is cumulative CFO (AED 107,273.7m). Memo: FCF (CFO − PP&E capex) = AED 102,979.2m.

| Use | Cumulative Amount (AED m) | Currency | % of CFO | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| Acquisitions & stake changes (net cash) | ~1,004 | AED | 0.9% | CIQ Financials_Annual → Cash Flow (Cash Acquisitions −2,500 FY22 / +657 FY24; Divestitures +1,231 FY23; NCI buy-in −392 FY25) |
| Buybacks | 0 | AED | 0.0% | FY2025 AR (IFRS), Note 27 (Share Capital); CIQ Historical Capitalization — no treasury purchases |
| Dividends (consolidated cash, incl. subsidiary NCI) | 25,236 | AED | 23.5% | CIQ Cash Flow (Common Dividends Paid); FY2025 AR, Note 32 + Statement of Changes in Equity |
| Capex (PP&E) | 4,295 | AED | 4.0% | CIQ Cash Flow (Capital Expenditure, FY21–FY25) |
| Investment property / development land (purchase) | 3,749 | AED | 3.5% | CIQ Cash Flow (Sale/Purchase of Real Estate) |
| Debt repayment (net of new issuance) | 9,312 | AED | 8.7% | CIQ Cash Flow (Total Debt Repaid 34,998 − Issued 25,686) |
| Net deployment into securities / term deposits | 18,792 | AED | 17.5% | CIQ Cash Flow (Invest. in Marketable & Equity Securities) |
| Retained as cash (net change in cash) | 46,929 | AED | 43.7% | CIQ Cash Flow (Net Change in Cash) |

**Read of the mix.** Over five years Emaar generated **AED 107.3bn of operating cash** and used it in a strikingly conservative pattern: it returned **AED 25.2bn as dividends (23.5%)**, paid down **AED 9.3bn of net debt (8.7%)**, spent only **AED 8.0bn on capex plus development land (7.5% combined)** — because an off-plan developer funds growth through customer pre-payments (unearned revenue rose ~AED 8bn/yr), not through fixed-asset capex — did **zero buybacks**, and spent almost nothing net on outside M&A (**0.9%** of CFO in cash). The single largest destination of cash was the balance sheet itself: **~AED 65.7bn (61%) of five-year CFO was retained as cash and term deposits/securities** (AED 46.9bn cash build + AED 18.8bn into financial assets). That is the defining capital-allocation choice — deleverage, distribute, and hoard — not build-at-any-cost. (Small reconciling items: other-investing inflows ~AED 7.6bn incl. JV dividends/asset sales, and other-financing outflows ~AED 5.0bn incl. leases/interest, close the residual.)

---

## 2. M&A Scorecard

Currency: AED. Emaar's growth engine is organic mega-development, not acquisition. Within the window there are two material deals plus a legacy tail; both material deals are **affiliate / related-party consolidations**, part- or fully equity-funded, and small relative to Emaar's ~AED 108bn equity.

| Deal | Year | Price | Disclosed Rationale / Synergy | Delivered? | Opportunity Cost (divestitures forced / focus lost / options foregone) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| **Dubai Creek Harbour** — buy Dubai Holding's 50% to take 100% | Aug 2022 | **AED 7.5bn** — half cash (~AED 3.75bn), half **newly-issued Emaar shares to Dubai Holding** (the controlling owner) | Consolidate flagship waterfront district; now hosts the AED 200bn masterplan, Dubai Square Mall & Dubai Mall expansion | **Partly / too-early.** DCH is now a core growth engine (management cites revenue "including DCH" of AED 36.4bn); **but no arm's-length valuation was disclosed to minorities**, and the seller = the controller who was paid in fresh Emaar stock (raising its own stake) | Dilution to the controlling owner; minority holders cannot verify the price was fair. This is a **related-party** deal, not third-party — the price was not set at arm's length | CIQ Direct Investments (snapshot 2026-06-28); FY2025 AR (IFRS), Note 2.1 (acquisitions), Note 33 (Related Party) |
| **Emaar Malls minority buy-in** (~13.7%) + delist | 2021 | **~AED 3.55bn**, all in Emaar shares (0.51 swap) | Take full control of the recurring mall/leasing annuity (Dubai Mall) | **Yes.** Leasing/retail now ~15% of revenue (AED 7.68bn FY25); Dubai Mall 110m+ visitors; recurring income consolidated | Mild dilution; removed a separately-listed pure-play the market could value on its own | CIQ Direct Investments (Size $967.6m ≈ AED 3.55bn); ciq_facts.json (segments); business-model/11 |
| Small stake consolidations (subsidiary acq. FY24 +AED 0.66bn net cash acquired; NCI buy-in FY25 AED 0.39bn) | 2024–25 | ~AED 0.4bn net | Routine step-ups in own subsidiaries | n/a (immaterial) | Negligible | FY2025 AR, Note 2.1; CIQ Cash Flow |
| **Legacy overseas expansion** (Emaar India/MGF 2010, Emaar Misr/Egypt 2007, Emaar Turkey 2009, Beverly West/US 2007) | pre-window | historical | Global diversification (2005–2012 era) | **Largely NO.** Charged **~AED 3.6bn of asset write-downs/restructuring FY2021–FY2023** (AED 0.67bn + 0.92bn + 2.01bn) and still carries live India litigation (ED / DDA-PDA disputes, contingent ~AED 0.6–1.2bn) | The visible cost of the pre-2013 empire-building era: capital and management focus diverted, now being cleaned up | CIQ Cash Flow (Asset Writedown & Restructuring line, FY21–FY23); FY2025 AR, Note 30 (Contingencies); management-governance/01 |

**Serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4).** **NOT tripped — RF-CAP-004 NOT emitted, M&A component NOT capped on this ground.** Emaar is not a serial acquirer: its capital engine is organic development; the two window deals are affiliate/related-party consolidations (one all-stock, one half-stock), each a low single-digit % of Emaar's ~AED 108bn value; and Emaar carries **net cash**, not acquisition debt near or above its own value. Cash spent on outside M&A was ~0.9% of five-year CFO. The base-rate warning that "most M&A destroys value" *is* visible historically — the ~AED 3.6bn of FY21–23 write-downs and live India litigation are the tail of the 2005–2012 overseas spree — but that value destruction is pre-window and now stopped.

**The real M&A charge is governance, not size.** The Dubai Creek Harbour deal is the item to watch: a large asset bought **from the controlling owner** and part-funded by **issuing new Emaar shares to that same owner**, with **no arm's-length valuation shown to minorities**. It is disclosed and had a clear strategic rationale (so RF-CAP-001 "vague rationale" is NOT triggered), but the pricing opacity is exactly the related-party channel that business-model/11 flags as the most material governance signal (IAS 24 election, severity 55/50) and that agents 04/06 own. Charged here as an opportunity-cost caveat on the M&A score; routed for RPT/ownership adjudication, not independently escalated to Critical.

---

## 3. Buyback Scorecard

| Year | Amount Spent | Currency | Shares Bought | Implied Avg Price | Share-Count Change | Offset SBC Dilution? | Price-Discipline Evidence | Judgment | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| FY2021–FY2025 | **0** | AED | **0** | **N/A — no buybacks** | Flat at **8,838,790k** since FY2023 (weighted-avg shares 8,838,790k); rose in 2021–22 from share-funded M&A | No material stock-comp to offset; no buyback either way | None to assess — zero repurchases | **No buyback discipline to score** — capital returned via dividends, not buybacks | FY2025 AR, Note 27 (Share Capital: "8,838,789,849 shares of AED 1 each"); CIQ Historical Capitalization |

**Read.** There were **no buybacks in the period**, so there is no implied average price and no price-discipline evidence in either direction. Share count is flat at 8,838.79m from FY2023 onward; the earlier step-up (2021–22) was **share-funded M&A** — the Emaar Malls swap and the Dubai Creek Harbour part-consideration issued to Dubai Holding — i.e., mild **dilution**, not a buyback, and it was not offset by any repurchase. Emaar does not run a large stock-compensation program, so there is no dilution treadmill to neutralise (RF-CAP-002 "buybacks not reducing share count" does NOT apply — there are no buybacks). Returning cash entirely through dividends rather than buybacks is consistent with a controller (the Government of Dubai, via Dubai Holding) that wants dividend cash flow. **Intrinsic-value discipline is not assessable from this module** — the valuation module has not run, so I do not judge whether the stock (P/E 5.9x, EV/EBITDA 4.0x at the 0th percentile of its own 16-quarter range per ciq_facts.json) is below fair value or whether the absence of buybacks is a missed opportunity.

---

## 4. Dividends & Organic Reinvestment

**Correction to the upstream read (primary-source, §4/§5).** Agents 01 and business-model/11 state the dividend was "held flat at AED 1.00/share across FY2023–FY2025." The audited **FY2025 AR Note 32** and **FY2024 AR Note 32** show otherwise: the parent dividend was **AED 0.50/share for FY2023** (50% of share capital, AED 4,419,395k) and **AED 1.00/share for FY2024 and FY2025** (100% of share capital, AED 8,838,790k each). The cash-flow line reconciles exactly — FY2024 cash dividends of AED 7,289,411k = parent AED 4,419,395k (FY2023 dividend) + subsidiary/NCI AED 2,870,016k. So the dividend **grew** (AED 0.25 FY22 → 0.50 FY23 → 1.00 FY24 → 1.00 FY25), which is a stronger, not weaker, distribution story than "flat" — but the flat-at-AED-1.00 claim is a misread and is corrected here.

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| Parent DPS trajectory | AED 0.25 (FY22) → 0.50 (FY23) → 1.00 (FY24) → 1.00 (FY25) | FY2023 AR Note 32 (0.50/share); FY2024 AR Note 32 (1.00/share); FY2025 AR Note 32 (1.00/share proposed) |
| Payout ratio (parent DPS ÷ EPS attributable) | FY2023 **38%** · FY2024 **65%** · FY2025 **50%** (falling as earnings outgrow the flat AED 1.00) | FY2025 AR, Note 29 (EPS); Note 32; earnings/01_historical-financials.md |
| Dividend coverage — FCF (CFO−capex) | Parent div (AED 8.84bn) covered **3.7x**; consolidated div (AED 11.18bn) covered **2.9x** by FY2025 FCF (AED 32.5bn) | CIQ Cash Flow, FY2025 |
| Dividend coverage — *normalised* operating FCF | ~**2.7x** parent / ~**2.1x** consolidated vs ~AED 23.6bn normalised FCF (strips ~AED 8bn presale-advance inflow, §15) | earnings/01_historical-financials.md |
| NCI dividend leakage (to subsidiary minorities, e.g. Emaar Development) | **~AED 2.3–2.9bn per year** (FY24 2,870m; FY25 2,340m) — a structural cash drain to minorities of listed subsidiaries | FY2025 AR, Statement of Changes in Equity ("Dividend and directors' bonus of subsidiaries") |
| Incremental ROIC (Δ NOPAT / Δ invested capital, FY21→FY25) | **~45–55% (derived, approximate)** — ΔNOPAT ≈ +AED 14.0bn (EBIT 6,554→22,552, tax-adjusted at UAE 9%); ΔInvested capital ≈ +AED 25–30bn (book equity+debt). *Well above* the ~11–12% cost of capital, but **cycle-aided** | *Inference, not from filings* — derived from CIQ EBIT + equity/debt; ROIC path 4.7%→13.7% per ciq_facts.json |

**Read.** The dividend is **growing, deeply covered, and funded by real cash** — parent payout ~50% of FY2025 earnings, covered ~3x by free cash flow and ~2x even on normalised operating cash, on a net-cash balance sheet. It is sustainable (RF-CAP-003 "dividends not covered by FCF" is NOT triggered — Green). Two caveats: (1) a structural **AED 2–3bn/yr leaks to the non-controlling holders of listed subsidiaries** (Emaar Development, Emaar Misr) before it reaches Emaar Properties shareholders; and (2) coverage is flattered by presale advances — on a mid-cycle basis (consensus models an earnings peak ~2029, then a −14.8% long-term decline) the deep cover would compress, though a flat AED 1.00 dividend would still be affordable. **Organic reinvestment is where the per-share value was actually created:** ROIC rose from 4.7% to 13.7%, and because growth is funded by customer advances (negative working capital) rather than fresh equity/debt, the return on *incremental* capital is very high (~45%+ derived) and comfortably above the cost of capital — but that is heavily cycle-aided (Dubai upcycle + legacy cheap land + a FY2025 tax one-off), so credit it as cycle-strong reinvestment, not proof of through-cycle skill.

---

## 5. Capital-Allocation Read

Over FY2021–FY2025 Emaar turned AED 107bn of operating cash into a **net-cash balance sheet, a dividend that quadrupled per share (AED 0.25→1.00) and is covered ~3x by free cash flow, and organic development returns that lifted ROIC from 4.7% to 13.7%** — capital was allocated to per-share value, and every classic value-destruction trap was avoided (no debt-funded acquisition spree, no buybacks at high prices, no uncovered dividend, no runaway capex). The **best use** was the combination of deleveraging to net cash and a growing, deeply-covered dividend, funded by high-return, customer-advance-financed development; the **worst use** was the **AED 7.5bn Dubai Creek Harbour purchase from the controlling owner (Dubai Holding), part-paid in newly-issued Emaar shares to that same owner with no arm's-length valuation shown to minorities**, sitting alongside ~AED 3.6bn of legacy overseas-expansion write-offs (FY21–23) that are the visible cost of the pre-2013 empire-building era. The **single biggest capital-allocation signal is a governance one, not a deployment failure**: the related-party capital-allocation channel that IAS 24 leaves largely unquantified under government control — today it is not extractive (distributions are pro-rata, returns are rising, the balance sheet is net cash), but it is the lever that would flip this from disciplined to value-destructive if a future asset were bought richly from the parent and funded by dilution. Two efficiency caveats keep it out of the top bucket: the record is heavily **cycle-aided** (consensus models an earnings peak ~2029), and **~61% of five-year operating cash now sits as cash and low-yield financial assets** — strategic optionality for the ~AED 65bn forward capex pipeline and the AED 200bn masterplan, but a drag on blended returns.

**Conclusion: value-creative / disciplined on the capital actually deployed — with material governance caveats (related-party asset-injection channel + government control) and a cycle-aided record.**

### Hard Self-Check
- Uses-of-capital sum shown as % of CFO (not just AED): each row carries % of cumulative CFO AED 107,273.7m; reconciled by executed Python. ✓
- M&A returns disclosed/computed, not assumed: DCH AED 7.5bn and Emaar Malls ~AED 3.55bn are disclosed prices; delivery judged on disclosed segment/revenue outcomes and disclosed write-downs — no assumed synergies. ✓
- M&A charged its opportunity cost (dilution to controller, legacy overseas write-offs/focus); serial-acquirer test run and NOT tripped (net cash, ~0.9% of CFO cash M&A) → RF-CAP-004 not emitted, M&A component not capped on that ground (§24 Filter 4). ✓
- Buyback average price stated as "N/A — no buybacks"; judged only on observable evidence (net share count, no SBC treadmill); intrinsic-value discipline explicitly marked not assessable pre-valuation. ✓
- Buybacks tested vs stock-based-comp dilution (net share-count change): flat since FY2023; mild pre-2023 M&A dilution; no SBC treadmill. ✓
- Incremental ROIC computed where data allows (~45–55%, derived, labelled inference and cycle-aided). ✓
- Verdict is per-share (EPS 0.52→1.99, DPS 0.25→1.00), not absolute size. ✓
- Multi-year history available → NOT single-period; no partial-data cap; caps checked and none applied. ✓
- Primary-source correction made where upstream disagreed (dividend AED 0.50 for FY2023, not flat AED 1.00), citing FY2023/FY2024/FY2025 AR Note 32; reconciled to cash flow. ✓
- Reconciled to ciq_facts.json (CFO, net cash, debt, cover, shares, ROIC) — no material gap; §15 note on CIQ levered-FCF vs CFO−capex. ✓
- Jurisdiction map applied (UAE/DFM, IFRS, AED; AR Notes, DFM intimations — no US-form assumptions). ✓
- No banned phrases (no naked "disciplined capital allocation" — every use paired with a cited per-share/coverage number). ✓

## management-governance / 03_incentives-and-compensation.md

_Source: `03_incentives-and-compensation.md`_

# Incentives & Compensation — EMAR

*Emaar Properties PJSC (DFM: EMAAR). Jurisdiction: UAE / Dubai Financial Market. Reporting standard: IFRS. Reporting currency: AED (dirham), pegged to USD at 3.6725 (so AED figures ÷ 3.6725 = USD). Fiscal year ends 31 December; latest audited year FY2025.*

*Compensation source (UAE proxy-equivalent, CLAUDE.md §27): the FY2025 Integrated Annual Report — Corporate Governance Report §3(c) (board remuneration proposal, approved at the AGM held 25 March 2026) and **Annex D — Senior Executive Employees** (actual salaries, bonuses and phantom-share entitlements paid for/during 2025). The CIQ "Compensation Summary" export carries only non-executive director fees (see §1 note); executive pay is taken from the audited AR (Tier-1, §4). `ciq_facts.json` holds no compensation field, so there is nothing to reconcile against it here.*

*Scope: this agent judges what the pay actually rewards — per-share value or size/empire. Ownership/insider behaviour (agent 04), board independence (agent 05) and full candor (agent 06) are referenced only as context.*

## 1. Compensation Structure

Reporting currency **AED**; period **FY2025** (year ended 31 Dec 2025). Phantom-share amounts are shown as **unit counts** (the hard fact from Annex D) with a clearly-labelled **indicative** AED value at ~AED 12.2/share (USD 3.32 × 3.6725, CIQ Comps 2026-06-28); the actual LTIP payout crystallises at the end-of-term share price and is subject to undisclosed vesting gates (§2).

| Executive | Base (salary & allowances) | Annual Bonus (cash) | Long-Term Incentive | Cash vs Equity Mix | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|---|
| **Group CEO** — Amit Jain | AED 5.28m | AED 9.00m | 533,778 phantom shares (2025 tranche; share-price-linked, 3-yr vest) ≈ AED ~6.5m indicative | ~25% fixed / ~43% variable cash / ~32% equity-linked → **~75% at-risk, ~32% share-price-linked** | FY2025 AR, Annex D (Senior Executive Employees) |
| **Group CFO** — Hesham Heikal (incumbent through 20 May 2026) | AED 2.18m | AED 1.38m | 151,793 phantom shares ≈ AED ~1.85m indicative | ~40% fixed / ~26% variable cash / ~34% equity-linked | FY2025 AR, Annex D |
| *Memo — Managing Director (founder)* — Mohamed Alabbar | AED 12.00m **+ AED 13.32m business travel/logistics** | **AED 36.00m** | **none — excluded from the phantom/LTIP plan** | **~100% cash / 0% equity-linked** | FY2025 AR, Annex D + CG Report §3(c)(2) |

*Currency/period restated: all figures AED, FY2025, from Annex D of the FY2025 Integrated Annual Report. USD equivalents at the 3.6725 peg: CEO cash AED 14.28m ≈ USD 3.89m; CEO total (incl. indicative phantom) ≈ AED 20.8m ≈ USD 5.7m; CFO total ≈ AED 5.4m ≈ USD 1.5m; MD total AED 61.32m ≈ USD 16.7m.*

**The new CFO is not in FY2025 pay.** Pawan Chindalia was promoted to Group CFO on 20 May 2026 (agent 01); FY2025 Annex D reflects the departed CFO Heikal. The abrupt, unexplained Heikal exit (agent 01, RF-MGT-001 watch) means the FY2025 CFO pay line will not recur as-is.

**CIQ director-fee export note.** The CIQ "Summary Compensation" file lists only **non-executive director fees** for FY2022–FY2025 (range AED 45,000–330,000; Vice-Chairman Ahmed Jamal Jawa highest at AED 295,000 in 2025, AED 330,000 in 2023; Chairman Bin Thaniah AED 215,000 in 2025) [CIQ Compensation Summary export, 2026-06-28]. Those per-director figures sum to ~AED 1.45m for 2025 — they capture the committee/meeting **attendance allowances** (CG Report §3(c)(3) = AED 1,450,000), **not** the full **AED 8.4m** non-executive board remuneration pool proposed for 2025 nor any executive pay. So the CIQ export understates board pay and omits executives entirely; the AR Annex D is the authoritative executive-pay read.

## 2. Performance Metrics

| Plan | Metric | Weight | Per-Share/Returns or Size-Based? | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| **Annual bonus** (CEO AED 9.0m; MD AED 36.0m; CFO AED 1.38m) | **Not disclosed.** CG Report states executive management is "subjected to a robust annual exercise of performance reviews directly linked to their remuneration"; the AGM approves the bonus amounts; the MD's AED 36m is stated to be "for his executive duties in 2025." No KPI, target or formula is published. | **Not disclosed** | **Cannot classify — metrics/weights undisclosed.** Functionally a board/AGM-approved discretionary cash bonus. Not shown to be returns-based; equally not shown to be revenue/EBITDA/deal-count-based. | FY2025 AR, CG Report §3(c) & §"Evaluation…"; Annex D |
| **Long-Term Incentive Plan (LTIP 2023–25)** — phantom shares (CEO 533,778; Exec Director 271,261; CEO Strategic Projects 166,361; CFO 151,793 units, 2025 tranche) | Cash-settled **Phantom Shares**; "the amount payable … in respect of any Phantom Shares is **linked to the share price of the Company**"; vests "on the last day of the 3-year term **based on the achievement of 3-year forward financial matrices**"; stated aim "to achieve **long-term shareholder returns**." | Vesting **matrices and weights not disclosed** (only the 3-year horizon and the share-price linkage of the payout) | **Per-share / returns-based *mechanism* (payout tied to the share price ≈ a TSR-style link), BUT the gating "financial matrices" are undisclosed** — so the design points at per-share value even though the specific hurdles cannot be verified. | FY2025 AR, Annex D (LTIP note + Phantom Shares table) |

**Classification summary.** One of the two variable plans — the 3-year phantom-share LTIP that covers the CEO, CFO and senior team — is built on a **per-share/returns mechanism** (payout linked to Emaar's share price). The other — the annual cash bonus, which is the single largest variable item (the CEO's AED 9.0m bonus exceeds his AED 5.28m salary; the MD's AED 36.0m dwarfs everything) — has **no disclosed metric at all**. Neither plan discloses metric **weights**. Note the size-based-risk item: the biggest single pay packet (the MD's AED 36m bonus) sits entirely outside the share-price-linked plan.

## 3. Pay vs Performance

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **CEO total pay (latest)** | FY2025: AED 5.28m salary + AED 9.0m bonus = **AED 14.28m cash**, plus 533,778 phantom shares (~AED 6.5m indicative) → **~AED 20.8m (~USD 5.7m)**. ~75% at-risk, ~32% share-price-linked. Not outsized for a ~USD 29bn-market-cap developer. | FY2025 AR, Annex D |
| **MD total pay vs stake** | **AED 61.32m (~USD 16.7m)** — salary AED 12.0m + AED 13.32m travel/logistics + AED 36.0m bonus — with **zero equity-linked pay**, for a founder-MD who owns **0.03%** of the shares (agent 01). ~3x the professional CEO's total, ~4x his cash. | FY2025 AR, Annex D + CG §3(c)(2); agent 01 (ownership 0.03%) |
| **Aggregate exec pay vs company** | Six named executives: ~AED 76.5m cash + AED 13.3m MD travel ≈ **AED 89.9m** (+ ~AED 13.7m indicative phantom). That is ~**0.18% of FY2025 revenue (AED 49,557m)** and ~**1.0% of the AED 8.84bn dividend** — immaterial to minorities in aggregate. The alignment issue is **structure**, not aggregate size. | FY2025 AR Annex D; ciq_facts.json (revenue); agent 01 (dividend AED 8.84bn) |
| **Pay vs peers (if available)** | No per-executive peer pay in the pool; UAE developer exec pay is generally disclosed only in aggregate. Web context: Aldar Properties aggregate **board** remuneration was AED 41.85m for FY2023 (with a 3-yr deferred, company-matched plan) — **not like-for-like** with Emaar's split disclosure. Notably, Emaar's Annex D gives **more** per-executive granularity (per-position salary+bonus+phantom units) than many UAE peers' aggregate-only board disclosure — a relative disclosure positive on *amounts*, though the *metrics* remain undisclosed. | Web: Aldar CG Report 2024 (FY2023), unverified 2026-07-10; FY2025 AR Annex D |
| **Pay-for-performance alignment** | The phantom-share LTIP genuinely ties the CEO/CFO/senior team to the share price over 3 years, and the CEO's mix is majority at-risk — a per-share link. Against that: the **largest packet (MD) has no share-price link and an undisclosed bonus basis**, and FY2025 pay rose alongside a **cycle-aided, FY2025-one-off-tax-flattered** result (agents 01/earnings) with no disclosed returns gate to confirm the bonus tracked per-share value rather than a strong market. | FY2025 AR Annex D; agent 01 (cycle-aid, one-off tax); earnings/04 |
| **Say-on-pay support (%)** | Mechanism present — the AGM (held 25 March 2026) approves FY2025 board remuneration (AED 8.4m non-exec, AED 36m MD bonus, AED 1.5m chairman). **Detailed vote tallies / scrutinizer results are not in the pool → Insufficient Data.** With the **Government-of-Dubai-controlled register (~29.73% Dubai Holding group)** approving the founder-MD's pay, approval is near-certain and is not an independent check. | Key Developments (AGM 2026-03-25); triage 00; agent 01 (control) |

## 4. Incentive Alignment Read

The pay has a **genuinely per-share spine for the professional managers**: a phantom-share LTIP whose payout is explicitly linked to Emaar's share price and vests over three years covers the CEO, CFO and senior team, and it sits alongside a dividend held at AED 1.00/share and a flat share count (no dilution, no buyback games — agent 11) — so the salaried CEO (~75% at-risk, ~32% share-price-linked) and CFO have a real share-price stake despite owning almost none of the company. **But the single biggest pay packet is the least aligned money in the building**: the founder-MD takes AED 61m (≈ USD 16.7m, ~3x the CEO), owns 0.03%, is **excluded from the share-price-linked LTIP**, draws a AED 36m bonus with **no disclosed metric**, plus a AED 13.3m travel/logistics line — and that package is waved through by a government-controlled register, not an independent vote. Because neither the annual bonus nor the LTIP discloses its **metric weights**, I cannot confirm the cash bonus rewards per-share returns rather than sheer scale. **Conclusion: mixed** — the mechanism points the CEO/CFO at per-share value, but the largest, most discretionary, least-transparent packet does not.

**Single biggest incentive signal:** the founder-MD is paid ~AED 61m/yr (3x the CEO) with **zero share-price-linked pay** and an **undisclosed bonus basis** while owning 0.03% — the biggest pay is the least aligned.

*Out-of-scope note: no fair value, probabilities, sizing or rating are produced — those belong to the valuation module / master synthesizer.*

## Incentive Alignment Score

*Not capped: the "no proxy / compensation disclosure" cap (max 50) does NOT bind — the CG Report + Annex D executive-pay disclosure is present (triage 00). The 50/100 below is a **substantive** score driven by thin metric disclosure and the founder-MD misalignment, not a data cap.*

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |

| Returns / per-share metrics | 13 | 25 | Phantom-share LTIP payout linked to share price (+) and per-share dividend held (+) [03-008, 03-014]; but annual bonus has no disclosed metric (−) and the biggest packet (MD) sits outside the plan (−) [03-005, 03-007] |
| Metric clarity and weights | 7 | 20 | No metric weights disclosed for either plan; bonus fully undisclosed, LTIP only names "3-year forward financial matrices" [03-007, 03-009] |
| Pay-for-performance alignment | 11 | 20 | CEO ~75% at-risk / share-price-linked LTIP (+) [03-002, 03-008]; MD AED 36m discretionary, no share link, FY2025 pay rose on a cycle/one-off-tax-flattered result with no disclosed returns gate (−) [03-004] |
| Long-term orientation | 9 | 15 | 3-year vesting phantom LTIP is genuinely long-term for the CEO/CFO/senior team (+) [03-008]; MD (biggest) is 100% annual cash (−) [03-005] |

| Minority-holder alignment | 4 | 10 | Government-controlled register approves the founder-MD's outsized pay; NRC not independent-majority (−) [03-010, 03-004]; per-share dividend / no dilution a mild (+) [03-014] |

### Hard Self-Check
- Comp structure (base / bonus / LTIP, cash vs equity) shown for CEO **and** CFO, plus MD memo — from FY2025 AR Annex D, not assumed. ✓
- Actual bonus/LTIP items extracted from the AR (Annex D + CG §3(c)); metric **weights** are not published — stated as undisclosed, not invented. ✓
- Each metric classified: annual bonus = undisclosed (cannot classify); LTIP = per-share/returns mechanism (share-price-linked) with undisclosed gates. ✓
- Pay magnitude placed vs company (0.18% of revenue) and vs a labelled, web-sourced peer (Aldar aggregate board, not like-for-like). ✓
- Say-on-pay: mechanism (AGM 25-Mar-2026) noted; vote tallies not in pool → Insufficient Data. ✓
- Jurisdiction map applied (UAE/DFM; CG Report + Annex D as proxy-equivalent; no US-form assumptions); no language gap (filings in English). ✓
- Partial-data cap check: "no proxy/comp disclosure" cap does **not** bind (comp disclosure present); 50/100 is substantive, not a cap. ✓
- Every material claim is a Universal Findings Table row (03-001…03-014); every non-NA row has evidence + as-of date; every Amber/Insufficient row has a follow-up; no printed-page invention (named AR sections/annexes used). ✓
- No banned phrases (no naked "aligned with shareholders" / "shareholder-friendly"); every alignment claim paired with a specific metric or number. ✓

## management-governance / 04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md

_Source: `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`_

# Ownership & Insider Behavior — EMAR

*Emaar Properties PJSC (DFM: EMAAR). Jurisdiction: UAE / Dubai Financial Market. Reporting standard: IFRS. Currency: AED (dirham, pegged ~3.6725/USD). Fiscal year ends 31 December. Latest audited year FY2025; latest quarter Q1 2026. Annual reports are filed in English — no translation gap. Single class of 8,838,789,849 ordinary shares of AED 1 each.*

*Scope note: this agent maps ownership (a static %), insider behaviour (the signal), pledging (a risk), and who controls the votes. It does not score compensation (agent 03), board independence (agent 05), or value the company (valuation module). Insider vs institutional data cross-checked to the FY2025 Annual Report primary source; the `ciq_facts.json` ownership fields (insider_net_activity, top_institutional_holders, institutional_ownership_trend) are all status "missing" — the CIQ ownership workbook was not exported — so the CIQ Public Ownership Summary RTF and the audited Annual Report carry the read. Ownership/insider data is therefore NOT missing (no partial-data cap): the UAE/DFM regime discloses board securities transactions annually in the Corporate Governance Report, and that disclosure is in the pool.*

## 1. Ownership

| Holder | % Held | Share Class | Bought or Granted? | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Founder / Managing Director — Mohamed Ali Alabbar (himself) | 0.031% (2,700,500 sh) | Ordinary (single class) | Legacy founder equity (held since founding 1997); **no purchase or sale in 2025** | FY2025 AR (IFRS), CG Report — Board securities-ownership & transactions statement, p.125 |
| Alabbar — relatives (spouse/children) | 0.004% (324,310 sh) | Ordinary | Legacy; no 2025 transaction | FY2025 AR, CG Report — Board statement, p.125 |
| Chairman — Jamal Bin Theniyah (himself) | 0.0004% (39,088 sh) | Ordinary | Legacy; no 2025 transaction | FY2025 AR, CG Report — Board statement, p.125 |
| **Other insiders / all directors (aggregate)** | **0.03% (3,063,898 sh)** | Ordinary | Held, **not recently bought**; LTIP is **Phantom Shares (cash-settled, non-dilutive)** — no real granted stock | FY2025 AR, CG Report p.125 (reconciles exactly to CIQ "Individuals/Insiders" 3,063,898); FY2025 AR, Remuneration/LTIP note |
| **Controlling bloc — Government of Dubai / Dubai Holding group** | **29.73%** (post May-2026) | Ordinary | State-held anchor; acquired/transferred, not granted | FY2025 AR, Annex I (5%+ holders), p.153; "two government-related entities collectively hold ~29.8%", Responsible Value Creation — Economic Performance; Key Developments (DFM), 2026-05-11 |
| — within bloc: Emirates Power Investment LLC (ex-ICD block, Dubai Holding vehicle) | 22.27% (1,968,605,691 sh) | Ordinary | Acquired May-2026 from ICD for est. **AED 23.9bn** | Key Developments (DFM), 2026-05-11 |
| — within bloc: EITL DIFC SPC 1 LTD / "DH 7 LLC" (Dubai Holding entity) | 7.46% (659,050,967 sh) | Ordinary | State-held | FY2025 AR, Annex I, p.153 |
| Largest institutional holders — Vanguard (2.70%), BlackRock (2.52%), JP Morgan AM, Baillie Gifford | ~18.45% (Institutions aggregate; 174 holders) | Ordinary | Open-market institutional | CIQ Public Ownership Summary (vendor, positions to May-31-2026) — labelled vendor read |
| Public free float (DFM) | ~70.2% | Ordinary | — | FY2025 AR, Responsible Value Creation — Economic Performance ("remaining c.70.2% held by public investors through a free float on the DFM") |

**Total insider (director) ownership: 0.03%** — de minimis. **Controlling government bloc: 29.73%** (single largest by ~4x the next holder). **Free float: ~70.2%.** By nationality (Annex H, p.152, 31 Dec 2025): UAE 48.39%, GCC 4.55%, Arab 0.96%, **Foreign 46.10%** — Emaar is fully open to foreign ownership (no binding foreign-ownership cap), and Foreign-Companies alone hold 43.51%.

**Reconciliation note (§5).** The CIQ Public Ownership Summary summary-table double-counts the transferred block: it still shows a stale "Sovereign Wealth Funds >5%" line of 22.27% (ICD, position-dated Dec-31-2025) **and** a "Corporations (Private)" bucket of 29.75% that already contains the same block re-badged under Dubai Holding (position-dated May-14-2026). The audited primary source is unambiguous — the government-related bloc is **29.73%, not ~52%** — so I use the Annual Report figure and treat the CIQ SWF line as a position-date artifact.

## 2. Insider Transactions (last 12 months)

| Insider | Buy / Sell | Amount | Date | Notes | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| All Board members (Alabbar, relatives, Bin Theniyah) | **None** | 0 | FY2025 | CG Report states "Net Amount of Transaction (Sale/Purchase): **None**" for every director — no open-market buying or selling | FY2025 AR, CG Report — Board securities-transactions statement, p.125 |
| Investment Corporation of Dubai (ICD, state affiliate) | Sell / transfer | 22.27% (1,968,605,691 sh), est. **AED 23.9bn** | 2026-05-11 | **NOT an open-market sale** — transferred to affiliated state vehicle Emirates Power Investment (Dubai Holding); group stake **rose** to 29.73%; **preceded a strong Q1 2026** (net income +34.7% YoY) — RF-OWN-003 NOT triggered | Key Developments (DFM), 2026-05-11 |
| Emirates Power Investment LLC (Dubai Holding) | Buy / acquire | 22.27%, est. AED 23.9bn | 2026-05-11 | Intra-government transfer; ~AED 12.15/sh, roughly market | Key Developments (DFM), 2026-05-11 |

**Net insider activity: neutral.** Directors made **zero trades** in FY2025 — no conviction buying, but no red-flag selling. The only large ownership move was an **intra-government block transfer** (ICD → a Dubai Holding vehicle), disclosed, at roughly market value, after which the government's combined stake *increased*. This is not open-market insider dealing and not a sale ahead of bad news. Distinguishing the categories the structure requires: there were **no open-market insider buys, no option exercises delivering real shares (the LTIP is cash-settled Phantom Shares), and no 10b5-1-equivalent pre-set sales** — the disclosed insider-transaction line is simply empty for FY2025.

**Institutional flow (Dec-31-2025 → Mar-31-2026, CIQ Public Ownership Summary — vendor):** net **+39.1m shares (~+0.4% of shares out)**, breadth roughly balanced (36 increased vs 35 decreased positions; 9 new vs 3 sold-out). Buyers: JP Morgan AM +37.0m, Baillie Gifford +13.7m, AllianceBernstein +4.3m. Sellers: Columbia −21.7m, JOHCM −5.9m, BlackRock −3.7m. The Vanguard Group Inc line going to 0 (−299.0m) is offset by +303.6m of new/reclassified positions (Vanguard Capital Management LLC still holds 238.6m) — it reads as an **intra-Vanguard entity/custodian reclassification, not a clean quality-holder exit** (flagged for monitoring, not scored as an exit).

## 3. Pledging & Encumbrance

| Insider | Shares Pledged | % of Their Holding | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| All directors (Alabbar, relatives, Bin Theniyah) | 0 | 0% | FY2025 AR, CG Report — Board ownership statement, p.125 (no encumbrance) |
| Government bloc (ICD / Dubai Holding) | 0 disclosed | 0% | FY2025 AR, Annex I; business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md (disqualifier #2 NOT triggered) |

**No pledging of insider or controller shares disclosed.** The only "pledge" in the filings is asset security — "Certain property, plant and equipment assets are pledged as security against interest-bearing borrowings" [FY2025 AR, Note (PP&E), p.~205] — which is asset collateral, **not share pledging**. Cross-reference: `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` disqualifier #2 (">50% promoter/insider shares pledged") = **N (0%)**. UAE/DFM has no promoter-pledge disclosure regime as in India, but the audited board statement carries no encumbrance and pledged control-group shares ÷ control-group holding = 0%. **Not a red flag.**

## 4. Control Structure

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Dual-class / super-voting | **None.** Single class of 8,838,789,849 ordinary shares at AED 1 par, issued = authorised, no treasury shares, one-share-one-vote (standard PJSC) | FY2025 AR, Note 27 (Share Capital), p.208 |
| Controlled-company status | **De facto government-controlled.** Dubai Holding group ~29.73% is the largest bloc by ~4x the next holder (largest independent holder Vanguard 2.70%); combined with Dubai Holding executives on the 9-member board and a dispersed ~70% float, the state has effective control despite a sub-50% stake | FY2025 AR, Annex I, p.153; CG Report — Board composition (9 members) p.125; board bios (Dubai Holding executives) |
| Shareholder bloc with board rights | **No golden share, no formal disclosed nomination or veto right / reserved-matter clause.** Control is exercised de facto via the ~30% stake plus board representation, not a special-rights instrument | FY2025 AR — no special-voting-rights disclosure found; Note 27 |
| Controlling-owner objective alignment *(Filter 6)* | **Fails the test — government control.** Dubai Holding / the Government of Dubai is the controller; the founder-MD (Alabbar, also Director-General of Dubai's Dept. of Economic Development) simultaneously chairs **Eagle Hills, a competing global developer**; and the IAS 24 government-related-entity exemption leaves the bulk of Dubai-government-ecosystem dealings (land, utilities, construction — e.g. the AED 7.5bn Dubai Creek Harbour purchase from Dubai Holding, 2022) unquantified | FY2025 AR, Annex I; Note 33 (Related Party — IAS 24 election); CIQ Board Members; business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md |

**Unaligned-owner test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 6) — RF-OWN-004 FIRED.** Emaar's controller is the **Government of Dubai** (via Dubai Holding group, 29.73%). This is structure (a) of the filter — government control — where the state pursues multiple non-per-share objectives (city masterplanning, strategic projects, directed capital into national developments). The evidence traces to pool filings: the FY2025 Annual Report names "two government-related entities collectively hold ~29.8%" and the May-2026 block transfer is a move *within* the state's own vehicles. Concrete alignment frictions already visible: (1) the IAS 24 exemption makes the largest related-party channel unquantifiable to a minority holder by construction; (2) the founder-MD chairs a **competing** developer; (3) board seats are held by Dubai Holding officers. This is a **structural cap, not a discount to arbitrage** — I lower the Control-structure-fairness component and apply the **shareholder-friendliness cap (max 55)** and **governance-risk floor (55)** per MODULE_RULES to the synthesis. **Value-trap note handed to valuation:** persistent cheapness under a government owner (EV/EBITDA ~4.0x, 0th percentile of its own 16-quarter range per `ciq_facts.json`) is not, on its own, a margin of safety — a state controller can keep a name cheap and keep it cheap; price the misalignment, do not assume it re-rates. Mitigants worth stating plainly: single share class (one-share-one-vote), dividends flow **pro-rata** to all holders (AED 1.00/share held FY2023–25, ~AED 8.84bn), net-cash balance sheet, and no *visible* extraction to date — the channel is a watch item, not a proven leak.

## 4A. Market Conduct

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Insider buys/sells vs subsequent results (timing) | **No adverse timing.** Directors made zero securities transactions in FY2025; there is no insider sale ahead of a weak print. The May-2026 government block transfer preceded a *strong* Q1 2026 (net income AED 5.00bn, +34.7% YoY) | FY2025 AR, CG Report p.125; Key Developments 2026-05-11 |
| Unusual price / volume before announcements | Not assessable from the pool — no intraday price/volume series available; nothing flagged under the CIQ "Potential Red Flags/Distress Indicators" category over the last 6 months | CIQ Key Developments (category selected; none returned) |
| Exchange clarification sought for a price move | **None disclosed** in the 6-month Key Developments log | CIQ Key Developments, last 6 months |
| Institutional (FII/MF) holding trend; any sharp high-quality-holder exit | Roughly **stable** Dec-25→Mar-26 (net +39.1m sh, ~+0.4%); balanced breadth; the Vanguard Group Inc → 0 line appears to be an intra-Vanguard reclassification (offset by +303.6m new positions), not a genuine exit | CIQ Public Ownership Summary — Ownership Activity & Top Buyers/Sellers (vendor) |

**No market-conduct red flag (RF-MKT-001 / RF-MKT-002) triggered.** Insiders did not sell before weak results (they did not trade at all), and no unusual pre-announcement price/volume or exchange clarification is in the pool. Trade-timing at intraday granularity is unavailable — stated, not guessed.

## 5. Ownership / Insider Read

Skin in the game is **near zero and static**: the people running Emaar own **0.03%** of it, the founder-MD's 2,700,500 shares are legacy 1997 equity (not bought with cash, and the LTIP pays in cash-settled Phantom Shares that deliver no real stock and no dilution), and **directors executed no trades in FY2025** — a neutral signal, neither conviction-buying nor red-flag selling. The dominant fact is **who holds the votes**: the **Government of Dubai (Dubai Holding group, 29.73%)** controls the company, the founder-MD chairs a **competing** developer, and the IAS 24 exemption keeps the state-ecosystem related-party channel unquantified — a structural minority-alignment risk (**RF-OWN-004**, shareholder-friendliness capped at 55, value-trap note to valuation). The offsetting comforts are real but do not remove the cap: a single one-share-one-vote class, **no share pledging**, ~70% free float with credible global institutions (Vanguard, BlackRock, Baillie Gifford), stable institutional breadth, and pro-rata dividends — so the biggest risk to a minority holder is not theft on today's evidence, it is a controller whose objective may not be per-share value.
