# Board & Shareholder Rights — EMAR

*Emaar Properties PJSC (DFM:EMAAR). Jurisdiction: UAE / Dubai Financial Market. Reporting standard: IFRS (as issued by the IASB). Currency: AED (pegged ~3.6725/USD). Fiscal year ends 31 December. Latest audited year FY2025. Annual report filed in English — no translation gap.*

*Scope: this agent judges whether the board is a real check on management AND on the controlling owner, and whether minority rights are intact. Compensation magnitude/alignment is agent 03's; ownership/insider behaviour and the formal RF-OWN-004 quantification are agent 04's; hard RPT disqualifier is owned by `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (found N). Cross-module inputs used: `management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md`, `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` (referenced via 01). Agent 04 (ownership) has not yet run in this folder — the government-owner structural read below is taken directly from primary filings and applied per CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 6.*

## 1. Board Composition

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Board size | **9 directors** (1 Executive, 8 Non-Executive; 1 female) | FY2025 AR (IFRS), CG Report §3 (Board Composition); Governance overview snapshot (9 Directors / 1 Executive / 5 Independent / 1 Female) |
| Independent directors (%) | **5 of 9 = 55.6% independent** by the company's own classification (Almulla, Abdulrazzaq, Alfalasi, Bin Meshar, AlHemeiri). Exceeds the UAE SCA one-third minimum. **Non-independent = 4** (Chairman Bin Theniyah, Vice-Chairman Jawa, MD Alabbar, and Dubai Holding CIO Omar Karim). | FY2025 AR, CG Report §3 (table: NE/I vs NE/NI vs E/NI) |
| Chair / CEO split? | **Yes — split.** Chairman = Jamal Bin Theniyah (**Non-Executive, Non-Independent**); the executive is Managing Director Mohamed Alabbar; Group CEO Amit Jain is management, not on the board. Roles separated, but the chair is **not independent** (co-founder of DP World; Dubai-government/ports pedigree). | FY2025 AR, CG Report §3; 01_management-and-track-record.md |
| Audit / Comp / Nominating committee independence | **Audit:** Bin Meshar (Independent — Chair), Almulla (Independent), Jawa (Non-Independent) → **2/3 independent, independent chair, 100% attendance, 5 meetings.** **Nomination & Remuneration:** Alfalasi (Independent — Chair), Abdulrazzaq (Independent), Jawa (Non-Independent) → **2/3 independent, independent chair, 95% attendance, 7 meetings.** Both compliant with UAE SCA (majority-independent, independent chair). **Caveat:** the Non-Independent Vice-Chairman (Jawa) sits on **both** oversight committees. | FY2025 AR, CG Report §5 (Audit), §6 (Nomination & Remuneration); Governance overview (attendance) |

**True (not just legal) independence — the core issue.** By UAE rules the board is majority-independent, but the "independence" is from *management*, not from the *controlling shareholder*. Emaar is government-controlled (Note 33: "partly owned by Investment Corporation of Dubai ('ICD'), an entity owned by the Government of Dubai … which has a significant influence over the Company"; Dubai Holding group ~29.73% after the May-2026 transfer). And essentially **every "independent" director is a Dubai-government official or an executive of a Dubai-government-controlled entity**: Alfalasi is Director-General of Dubai Government Human Resources; Bin Meshar is Secretary-General of the Supreme Legislation Committee (Dubai government); AlHemeiri is CEO of Dubai Digital Government Establishment; Abdulrazzaq is Group COO/CHRO of **Emirates NBD** (ICD-controlled); Almulla is a board member of **Emirates NBD** and Vice-Chairman of Emirates Islamic Bank (both government-linked). So the directors who are "independent of management" are not independent of the **government owner** — the board is structurally aligned with the controller, not with minorities. [FY2025 AR, CG Report §3 (director affiliations); Note 33; Public Ownership Summary]

The five Independents are comparatively fresh: Almulla & Abdulrazzaq 5 years (2021); Alfalasi, Bin Meshar, Karim 2 years (2024); AlHemeiri <1 year (July 2025). Refreshment happens on the *independent* bench; the controlling core is entrenched. | FY2025 AR, CG Report §3 (Date & Term of Appointment) |
| Overboarding / staleness signs | **Material overboarding at the top.** MD Alabbar simultaneously chairs a **directly competing global developer, Eagle Hills**, plus chairs **Americana Restaurants (ADX)**, and is founder/director of **Noon.com**, executive board member of listed **Emaar Development**, and holds numerous other chairs/directorships. Vice-Chairman Jawa sits on many boards (Starling, Emaar Development, RAK Petroleum-prior, etc.). ## 3. Related-Party Transactions & Group Leakage

*Denominators (FY2025, AED m): Revenue 49,557; PAT attributable to owners 17,599; Net worth (total equity) 107,676 (common equity 94,278). RPT figures from audited Note 33 (AED '000 in filing, shown here in AED m).*

| RPT Type | Amount | % of Revenue | % of PAT | % of Net Worth | Arm's Length? | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Sales to related parties (rental + leasing/retail income from associates/KMP) | ~AED 99.5m | 0.20% | 0.57% | 0.09% | Mgmt: "normal course … terms agreed between parties"; Audit Committee reviews/approves | FY2025 AR, Note 33 |
| Purchases from related parties (cost of revenue + property-development expenses) | ~AED 137.0m | 0.28% | 0.78% | 0.13% | Same — "commercial terms" | FY2025 AR, Note 33 |
| SG&A + finance costs paid to related parties | ~AED 239.8m (SG&A 84.6 + finance cost 155.2) | 0.48% | 1.36% | 0.22% | Same | FY2025 AR, Note 33 |
| Loans / advances / guarantees to related parties | **None material disclosed** (other assets/receivables/deposits/prepayments to KMP & related = AED 16.3m) | 0.03% | 0.09% | 0.02% | — | FY2025 AR, Note 33 |
| Royalty / brand / management fees to promoter (government) group | **None disclosed** (Emaar owns its own brand; no fee to ICD/Dubai Holding) | — | — | — | NA | FY2025 AR, Note 33 |
| Outstanding receivables from related parties | AED 17.5m total (trade/unbilled 0.8 + 0.4; other 16.3) | 0.04% | 0.10% | 0.02% | — | FY2025 AR, Note 33 |
| (Memo) Payables **to** associates/JVs & KMP | AED 2,366m (assoc/JV 2,067; KMP/other 299) — **Emaar owes them; declining from AED 2,918m in 2024** | 4.8% | 13.4% | 2.2% | Operating balance, not leakage out | FY2025 AR, Note 33 |
| (Memo) Compensation of key management personnel | AED 129.2m (short-term 100.9 + LTIP 26.6 + EOSB 1.7); 2024: 131.2m — **flat/declining** | 0.26% | 0.73% | 0.12% | Disclosed aggregate | FY2025 AR, Note 33 |

**Read on leakage.** On the **disclosed** figures, related-party dealing is small and pointed the safe way: RP income ~0.35% of revenue, RP expenses ~0.76% of revenue (~1.2% of total expenses), receivables *from* related parties trivial (AED 17.5m), payables *to* JVs declining, and key-management pay flat. No loans/guarantees to related parties, no royalty/brand fee to the government owner. `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` tested the hard RPT bar and found **N** (0.35% of revenue / 1.2% of expenses — far below the 25% disqualifier). **The problem is what is *not* quantified.** Under the **IAS 24 government-related-entity exemption**, Emaar "has elected not to disclose" its transactions with the wider ICD/Dubai-government ecosystem — explicitly **"financing and operational (power, utilities, land purchases, infrastructure services, construction services etc.)"** — stated only as "commercial terms." These are the economically *large* flows, and Emaar's entire model rests on acquiring cheap Dubai land, much of it from the government sphere. So a minority holder cannot see whether the "cheap land" edge is an arm's-length purchase or a controller subsidy that could be repriced. This is a **disclosed, standard, legal election for a state-controlled issuer — not opacity in the language/§27 sense and not a threshold breach** — but it removes visibility over the single biggest potential value-transfer channel. Routed to agent 06 (disclosure completeness); it caps the group-leakage-protection sub-score but does **not** fire a hard RPT red flag on an unquantified amount (conservative default, §4).

**Founder-MD conflict (self-dealing channel).** MD Alabbar chairs **Eagle Hills**, a directly competing developer, while **chairing Emaar's Investment Committee** (which reviews Emaar's new investments, feasibility and financing — 9 meetings in 2025, the most active committee). The AR requires conflict disclosure and recusal, but **no separation protocol for Emaar↔Eagle Hills deal-flow is disclosed** to minorities. No actual Emaar↔Eagle Hills transaction is in the pool, so this is flagged as an **Amber** conflict channel (High materiality, unresolved), routed to agents 04/06 — not a fired RF on current evidence.

## 4. Takeover Defenses & Voting Rights

| Feature | Present? | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Poison pill | **No** | Not a UAE/DFM market instrument; none disclosed. | FY2025 AR (no rights plan disclosed) |
| Classified / staggered board | **No** | All 9 seats carry a unified term; board fully elected 22 April 2024. Whole-board election favours minorities over a staggered structure. | FY2025 AR, CG Report §3 (common appointment dates) |
| Dual-class / unequal voting | **No** | Single class of ordinary shares; 8,838,789,849 shares, one-share-one-vote. | Public Ownership Summary (single "Common Stock Equivalent" line); FY2025 AR |
| Majority vs plurality director voting | Cumulative voting (statutory) | UAE PJSC baseline: **cumulative voting for board elections** (a shareholder's votes = shares × seats, castable for one nominee) — a **minority-protective** feature that can help a bloc win a seat. Ordinary resolutions pass by majority of shares present. | *Inference from UAE Commercial Companies Law (jurisdiction norm), not a company-specific disclosure — labelled* |
| Shareholder ability to call meetings / act by written consent | Limited (statutory) | No written-consent-in-lieu mechanism (UAE requires actual general assemblies). Minorities may requisition a general assembly / add agenda items only at the UAE statutory threshold (≈10%+). In practice the government controller (~29.73%) sets the agenda. | *Inference from UAE CCL (jurisdiction norm) — labelled*; Public Ownership Summary (control block) |

| Dilution history | **Benign — no equity dilution.** Shares outstanding flat at **8,838.789849m every year** (FY2020–FY2025). No preferential allotment, QIP, warrants, or rights issue disclosed. The executive LTIP is **phantom (cash-settled) shares** (Annex D) — non-dilutive to minorities. Dec-2025 General Assembly approved a **debt** programme, not equity. | CIQ Financials → Historical Capitalization (Shares Out.); FY2025 AR, CG Report §3(g)/Annex D; Note (General Assembly, Dec 2025) |

*No votes-against or proxy-advisor data exists in the pool, so RF-SHR-001 (high votes-against) cannot be tested — stated as a data gap, not a clear. RF-SHR-002 (controversial dilution) is **not** triggered: zero equity dilution and a cash-settled LTIP.*

## 5. Minority-Shareholder Protection Read

On paper the board ticks the UAE boxes — 55.6% independent, an independent-chaired audit committee (2/3 independent, 100% attendance, reviewing every related-party deal), an independent-chaired nomination & remuneration committee, one-share-one-vote with statutory cumulative voting, no poison pill, no staggered board, no dual-class, and no share dilution — so it is **not** a case of classic management entrenchment. But box-ticking is the wrong test here: **the board is not independent of the party that actually controls Emaar.** The Government of Dubai controls the company (~29.73% via Dubai Holding/ICD, with Note 33 confirming "significant influence"), and every nominally "independent" director is a serving Dubai-government official or an executive of a government-controlled entity, with the controller's own Group CIO seated at the table — a board built to serve the owner, not to check it for minorities. The single biggest self-dealing/leakage risk is the **IAS 24 exemption that leaves the largest related-party flows — land bought from, and utilities/construction/financing paid to, the government ecosystem — entirely unquantified**, compounded by a founder-MD who chairs a directly competing developer (Eagle Hills) while chairing Emaar's most active committee (Investment). Per CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 6 (RF-OWN-004, structurally unaligned government owner — formally owned by agent 04), a board of the controller's own appointees cannot offset an owner whose objective is Dubai's development rather than per-share value; minority protection is therefore **weak / entrenched** — entrenchment of the *controller*, not of management. **Conclusion: weak / entrenched.**

*Out-of-scope note: no valuation, probabilities, or rating produced — those belong to the valuation module / master synthesizer.*

- Takeover defenses & voting standards inventoried — no pill / no staggered board / no dual-class; one-share-one-vote; statutory cumulative voting; zero dilution; cash-settled LTIP. ✓
- §24 Filter 6 applied: government controller confirmed from primary filings → RF-OWN-004, shareholder-friendliness capped ≤55, minority protection "weak / entrenched," value-trap note routed to valuation; formal quantification left to agent 04. ✓
- Jurisdiction map applied (UAE/DFM, SCA CG Report, Note 33, AGM); no US-form assumptions; UAE statutory voting labelled as jurisdiction inference, not company disclosure. ✓
- Data gaps marked Insufficient (AGM votes-against / proxy-advisor coverage), not guessed. ✓

## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

_Source: `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`_

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — EMAR

*Emaar Properties PJSC (DFM: EMAAR). Jurisdiction: UAE / Dubai Financial Market. Reporting standard: IFRS. Currency: AED (dirham, pegged to USD at ~3.6725). Fiscal year ends 31 December. Latest audited year FY2025; latest quarter Q1 2026 (released 11 May 2026). Annual reports are filed in English — no translation gap, and no home-language disclosure is treated as opacity (CLAUDE.md §27).*

**Partial-data rule applied up front.** The pool holds **no verbatim earnings-call transcript and no shareholder letter.** The two "Earnings Call Summary" files are **FAB Securities sell-side notes** ("Earnings Call Insight" 3Q25 / 4Q25, carrying Rating BUY and a Target Price) — an analyst's paraphrase of the call, not management's own words under questioning. A sell-side note **and** a company press release both fail the verbatim test, so **tone under questioning, Q&A-evasiveness, and owning-the-miss live are Not assessable** from them. Those components are marked **Insufficient Data** and the Disclosure-Candor score is **capped at 65**. What *is* assessable, and used here: the audited annual reports (FY2023–FY2025 MD&A and notes — primary), the company's own earnings **press releases** (primary written communications, Q3'25 / Q4'25 / Q1'26), and the `earnings/06_earnings-quality` cross-check on non-GAAP aggressiveness (present — no cross-check gap).

---

## 1. Owning the Misses

Emaar issues **no formal numeric (revenue/EBITDA/EPS) guidance** [earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md], so most "misses" are shortfalls against **Street consensus**, not against a bar management set itself — there is less for management to "own" or dodge, but also less accountability. The checkable operational promises (sales, backlog, dividend, deliveries) were **mostly kept** [01_management-and-track-record.md]. The genuine soft spots and how the *written* record handled them:

| Miss (from 01 / earnings) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Q4'25 gross/EBITDA-margin fade** — Q4'25 EBITDA margin −251 bps YoY to 54.2%, gross margin −218 bps YoY to 55.4% (0.01 bp = 1/100th of a percent); guided "low 50s sustainably" | Company Q4/FY2025 **press release does NOT flag the decline** — it attributes profit to "operational efficiencies and **healthy margins across all business lines**." The −251 bps fade and the "low-50s fading further" path appear only in the sell-side call summary and the detailed data, not in Emaar's own headline framing. | **Obscured in the promotional layer** (disclosed in the detailed pack / notes) | Q4/FY2025 press release, 12 Feb 2026, "Profitability"; FAB Securities "Earnings Call Insight" 4Q25 (unverified); ciq_facts.json (margin_trend) |
| **March 2026 hospitality softness** — UAE hotel occupancy 69% in Q1'26 vs 82% full-year FY2025 | Q1 2026 **press release owns it plainly**: "performance in March was affected by the **ongoing regional situation**"; also flags adverse-weather disruption. Specific segment, specific cause. | **Owned** | Q1 2026 press release, 11 May 2026, "Hospitality" |
| **Recurring revenue-timing misses vs consensus** — Q1'26 revenue −2.4%, Q2'25 revenue −4.6% (percentage-of-completion — revenue booked as construction progresses, so quarter-to-quarter timing is lumpy) | No company bar to miss (no guidance). Press releases headline the **true YoY facts** (Q1'26 revenue +23%) and do not mention the vs-consensus shortfall — neither hidden nor acknowledged; the profit line beat and is what management leads with. | **Neither owned nor hidden** (no self-set bar; verbal handling Not assessable — no transcript) | earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md; Q1 2026 press release |
| **FY2022 down year** — the only miss year: revenue −4% / GAAP-EPS −6% vs consensus; revenue fell YoY (AED 27.9bn→24.9bn) | **Not assessable** — no FY2022 press release or transcript in the pool. Adjacent evidence of conservatism: FY2021–FY2023 carried genuine impairments/doubtful-debt provisions (AED 0.4–1.0bn/yr) that *depressed* earnings rather than being avoided [earnings/06 §5]. | **Not assessable** (no primary FY2022 communication in pool) | ciq_facts.json (surprise_history); earnings/06_earnings-quality.md §5 |
| **Forward earnings deceleration** — consensus models a peak ~2029 then decline; LT-growth consensus −14.8% | Management keeps signaling growth ("backlog will continue to grow or remain stable **unless a sustained slowdown in sales**") — a **conditional** caveat, but the multi-year fade is a Street construct management does not adopt. | **Mildly caveated, not owned** | FAB 4Q25 summary (unverified); ciq_facts.json (consensus_view); earnings/04 |

**Read:** genuinely mixed. The one clear own-up (March hospitality, with a named cause) and the one clear soft-pedal (the Q4'25 margin fade framed as "healthy margins" in the company's own release) sit side by side. The pattern: **the audited/mandatory layer discloses the hard facts; the voluntary promotional layer does not foreground them.** How management handled misses *verbally, under analyst questioning,* cannot be judged — no verbatim transcript.

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

This is Emaar's strongest candor positive. The cross-check from `earnings/06` is unambiguous: **no adjusted EPS, no adjusted net income, no restructuring or stock-comp add-backs** — the company reports straight audited IFRS EPS.

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | **None on the P&L.** No adjusted-earnings measure exists. The only "recurring one-off" is a **cash-flow** item — the customer-advance inflow (cash buyers pre-pay for off-plan homes) that flatters CFO/FCF — surfaced and normalised by the earnings module, not a management adjustment. **RF-DISC-002 NOT triggered.** | earnings/06 §4, §5 |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | The only non-GAAP figure is a company-defined **"EBITDA"** (rough operating cash profit) of AED 25.6bn vs CIQ standardized EBITDA AED 24,132m — a **~AED 1.5bn / ~6% optimistic gap** because it folds in net finance income (~AED 2.0bn) + share of JV results. It reconciles transparently to profit-before-tax; it is not used to manufacture an earnings number. Audited IFRS EPS AED 1.99 is reported straight. | earnings/06 §4, §7; Q4/FY2025 press release |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | **Direction runs conservative, not aggressive.** FY2021–FY2023 impairments/provisions *depressed* reported earnings; they were not reversed aggressively to flatter the present. Investment property is held at **cost** (no fair-value gains inflating profit); stock comp is negligible (AED 1.68m). Earnings-quality score **81/100** — cash-backed even after stripping the advance tailwind. | earnings/06 §5, §8, §9 |

The one honest asterisk is not an *earnings* adjustment but a *cash* one: reported FCF (free cash flow = operating cash − capex) of ~AED 31.0bn LTM overstates steady-state cash by ~27% versus normalised ~AED 22.6bn, because the customer-advance build and a corporate-tax payment lag are cyclical tailwinds. Management does not headline FCF, so it makes no misleading FCF claim — but neither does it flag the cyclicality of its reported cash generation.

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

The **mandatory** disclosure is above-average and granular: three IFRS-8 segments with segment profit and geographic split (Real Estate 80% / Leasing-Retail 15% / Hospitality 5%; UAE 93% of revenue), full non-controlling-interest (NCI — the slice of consolidated profit owned by outside shareholders of listed subsidiaries) attribution (owners of parent AED 17,599m vs NCI AED 4,726m = 21.2% of net profit), the India Enforcement Directorate contingency quantified (AED 190m, ~0.2% of assets), the UAE corporate-tax step-up explained, and the KPIs that actually matter for a developer — property sales (the leading indicator), revenue backlog, land bank, deliveries, occupancy, absorption rates — all reported consistently [FY2025 AR notes; business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md; ciq_facts.json]. The single material transparency gap is the **IAS 24 government-related-entity election**: Emaar discloses only ~0.35% of revenue / 1.2% of expenses as related-party and elects **not to itemise the bulk of its dealings with the Dubai-government / ICD ecosystem** (land purchases, utilities, construction, power) — the very channel through which the AED 7.5bn Dubai Creek Harbour asset was bought from parent Dubai Holding in 2022. This is a disclosed, IFRS-permitted election (not opacity, not a language gap), but for a state-controlled issuer it leaves the largest related-party channel unquantified to a minority holder. No disclosure got **less** transparent over time — no KPI was dropped or redefined after underperformance (RF-MGT-003 not triggered) — but the abrupt Group-CFO exit (effective 20 May 2026, announced 22 May, **no reason disclosed**) is a completeness/timeliness ding on the executive-change axis.

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

The worst recent operational spot is the Q1'26 quarter (revenue a touch light vs consensus; March hospitality soft): here management's **written** tone is **mixed** — the release owns the March hospitality softness with a specific cause ("affected by the ongoing regional situation") yet wraps the quarter in promotional framing ("strong start," "well-positioned," "disciplined capital allocation," "prudent capital allocation," "resilient"), and the founder's quote pivots to **macro-political praise** of UAE government stability rather than any Emaar-specific accountability. The Q4'25 release, covering the margin-fade quarter, is more one-sided — "record," "highest-ever," "healthy margins across all business lines" — and does not surface the −251 bps EBITDA-margin decline in its own words. **Whether the tone is candid or evasive in the live Q&A is Not assessable** — the pool has no verbatim transcript, only a sell-side paraphrase, which reflects the analyst's selection, not management's answers under questioning.

## 5. Candor Read

**Mixed.** The accounting *substance* is genuinely clean and points toward candid — Emaar reports straight audited IFRS EPS with **no adjusted-earnings engineering**, its one-off history runs conservative (past impairments depressed, not flattered, earnings), and the mandatory notes disclose the hard facts (margin-by-segment, 21% NCI leakage, contingencies, tax step-up); the single biggest candor signal is that the most common tool of managerial deception — a flattering "adjusted" number — is simply **absent** here (`earnings/06` = 81/100). Pulling the other way: the *voluntary* layer is promotional and does not own the fades (the Q4'25 margin decline framed as "healthy margins"), the **IAS 24 election leaves the largest related-party channel unquantified**, there is **no formal numeric bar** to be held to, and the **verbal record is unverifiable** (no transcript). The routed integrity signal was **cleared** by agent 01 (RF-MGT-005 not emitted; no unresolved adverse integrity signal), so it does **not** compound against the trust read — but the government-control / founder-competitor / IAS 24 alignment overhang keeps this short of "high-trust." Net: management can broadly be trusted on the audited numbers, less so on the framing around what is going wrong. Verdict: **mixed.**
